```
---
name: clip-analyzer
description: "Clip analyzer at ~/Projects/clip-analyzer (port 8570, launch name clip-analyzer): upload a clip, per-frame camera motion (2D + 3D labels), whips, lighting; video plays with the analysis in sync; feedback buttons save verdicts"
metadata:
  type: project
---

Built 14 Nov 2026 with Nick for studying reference clips for Blender remakes (first clip: "My Mom Cracked The Dinosaur").
`scan.py` (OpenCV, every frame, caption band masked) -> `build_data.py` -> `web/index.html` served by `server.py`
(127.0.0.1:8570). `./verify.sh` = 19 checks (ten known 3D camera moves on a synthetic clip, upload off switch
`CLIP_UPLOAD=0`, upload pipeline, feedback save). Clips live in `clips/<id>/` (gitignored).

Sound (added 14 Nov): demucs voice split (~/.venvs/demucs, model re-downloaded with Nick's ok), `sound.py` finds whoosh / crack / hit / thud by signal shape on the no-voice track, `captions.py` reads burned-in captions with Apple's text reader through `ocr.js` (osascript; the Swift compiler on this Mac is broken). The AudioSet tagger (`label_sound.py`) heard nothing useful on this clip and is not in the pipeline. `CLIP_SOUND=0` skips the sound steps.

State on 14 Nov: 3D labels (dolly, truck, orbit, lens zoom) are proven on synthetic clips only; on real footage they
are unverified and flicker. Feedback verdicts are saved to `clips/<id>/feedback.jsonl` but nothing tunes from them yet.
Nick's old 1/6 s analysis (sound, captions, sheets; see [[stimulus-every-sixth]]) is not merged in yet. Nothing committed.
Research on stronger 3D tools (ViPE, MegaSaM, COLMAP) was done by a subagent and is not verified first-hand.
Related: [[blender-engine]], [[reuse-nicks-tools-first]].
```

```
# Run 021 review: the reference's camera moves on our two characters (14 Nov 2026)

Test: does the clip analyzer's reading of "My Mom Cracked The Dinosaur" (0 to 12.6 s) hold enough to
re-create its camera work in Blender? Each of the 13 shots got a hand-set start camera; every frame
after that was driven only by the analyzer's numbers (motion_0_12.json). Rendered on the shared
RTX 4090 pod: 379 frames at 360x640, 16 samples, 3 min 25 s (OptiX). The clip analyzer was then run
on our render with the same settings as on the reference (review/compare.py, review/compare.json).

## Measured (analyzer on ours vs analyzer on the reference, same frames)
- Zoom curve correlation 0.86, pan curve correlation 0.93 (per frame, 379 frames).
- All 9 reference zooms of 20% or more go the same way in ours:

| time | reference | ref zoom | ours |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.00 s | pull out | -68.1% | -65.0% |
| 1.63 s | push in | +35.4% | +36.1% |
| 2.37 s | pull out | -20.1% | -16.6% |
| 4.07 s | dolly in | +289.8% | +329.1% |
| 5.87 s | push in | +24.9% | +6.2% |
| 6.13 s | dolly in | +48.1% | +22.5% |
| 8.00 s | dolly out | -77.2% | -65.0% |
| 10.97 s | move left | -28.9% | -26.5% |
| 11.33 s | pull out | -29.4% | -46.8% |

- Brightness after two light cuts: 0.24 average over the 13 shot stills vs 0.26 for the reference.

## Still wrong
1. Whips: 36 reference frames are whips; the analyzer reads only 2 of ours as whips. Ours turn
   too little blur into the picture (shutter 0.5, one key per frame) and cut hard to the next shot,
   where the reference smears through. Next question: whip in AND out (the next shot starts mid-whip),
   longer shutter on whip frames.
2. Orbit at 0.7 to 1.6 s: the reference circles to the boy's 3/4 back; ours stays in front. The
   analyzer's 3D reading is "unsure" on 224 of 379 frames (15 of the 27 orbit frames), and the driver
   turns an orbit only by the size of the picture's sideways slide, so the orbit comes out far too small. Next question: read orbit angle from the subject staying put while the background slides.
3. 5.87 to 6.7 s (frames 176-198) the push reads +84.9% in the reference but +30.1% in ours, although
   all 23 frames are tracked and the driver applied every reading. Cause not found yet. A guess to
   test, not a finding: the driver dollies toward one aim point, and a dolly enlarges near and far
   things by different amounts, so the frame-wide size change the analyzer reads can come out smaller.
4. S10 dolly out passes behind the boy's head at the end (one shot of orange hair fills the frame).
5. Characters do not move (Mom does not slap the leg or drag the boy); Mom is still the placeholder.

## Files
review/side_by_side.mp4 (reference left with its sound, ours right), review/sbs_sheet.jpg,
review/whip_sheet.jpg, review/compare_stills_v3.jpg, review/compare.json, review/compare.py.
Our render is also in the clip analyzer as clip "run021-ours" for frame-by-frame viewing.

## Step 3, 14 Nov 2026: the opening shot with the camera solved from the reference (COLMAP)
The 2D readings could not make an orbit (0 of 55 orbit frames, 7% sideways travel). So the reference's
frames 0-54 were solved with COLMAP 4.2.1 / pycolmap (BSD licence, read from its LICENSE file) on the box:
55 of 55 frames, 0.79 px mean reprojection error, lens 36.1 mm. build.py `SOLVED=solved_s1.json` puts
that path on our boy (origin = solved points on the reference boy's hair, forward = toward the first
frame's camera, scale from the curls' width x `SOLVED_SCALE` 0.63, measured from the first render).

| check (frames 0-54) | reference | v2 (driven) | solved |
|---|---|---|---|
| camera round the boy, frames 9-48 (COLMAP on each video; ours measured on the first solved render, before the scale fix, which does not change angles) | 93.3 deg | not measured | 91.8 deg |
| reference orbit frames our analyzer also reads as orbit | 15 | 0 |  ...[cut]
```

```
"""Render several variants of one setting as fast clay previews and tile them next to a
reference crop, so shapes can be compared side by side in one image.

Usage: python tools/variants.py SCRIPT VIEW REFERENCE_CROP OUT.png "label=settings" ...
Each settings string is passed to SCRIPT in the COLLAR environment variable.
"""
import os
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
import time

from PIL import Image, ImageDraw

script, view, reference, out = sys.argv[1:5]
variants = [a.split("=", 1) for a in sys.argv[5:]]
tiles = [("REFERENCE", Image.open(reference).convert("RGB").resize((360, 360)))]
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
    for label, settings in variants:
        path = os.path.join(tmp, f"{len(tiles)}.png")
        started = time.time()
        env = dict(os.environ, CLAY="1", COLLAR=settings.replace(";", ","), OUT=path)
        subprocess.run([sys.executable, script, view], env=env, check=True,
                       stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL)
        tiles.append((f"{label} ({time.time() - started:.0f}s)", Image.open(path).convert("RGB")))
sheet = Image.new("RGB", (len(tiles) * 370, 400), (20, 20, 20))
draw = ImageDraw.Draw(sheet)
for i, (label, im) in enumerate(tiles):
    sheet.paste(im, (i * 370, 36))
    draw.text((i * 370 + 8, 10), label, fill=(235, 235, 235))
sheet.save(out)
print(out)
```
</details>

### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T10:52

Your idea works, and we already have what makes it a game: the clip analyzer can score any zoom take against a reference. Earlier I guessed the wrong "zooms", so ignore my last status for now.

**Three ways to play it:**
- **Zoom Duel (scored):** the animators get the room, the two characters, the script and the finished animation. They fire zoom and whip buttons live while it plays, then see their take next to the reference with a score. Example: "line 3, push in on the boy's face at 1.63 s", scored on the zoom direction and size we already measure.
- **Prop hunt (playful):** the room is packed with props. Each script line hides a target, so "she drags him" means find the rope and zoom to it on the beat. Hits unlock bonus shots, and misses are just funny.
- **Director's cut (free):** no score, just a sandbox. Take it and the animators remix it into a clip.

**My pick: Zoom Duel with the prop hunt as its levels.**
- **Level 1:** add 3 zooms.
- **Level 2:** a full 12 s clip.
- **Level 3:** hidden targets in the clutter.
- **Easy mode:** the buttons snap to the target.

🔴 **NEEDED:** do you want a score against a reference, or a free sandbox first? **My pick is scored,** because the checker already exists. I can draw the screen as a page if you'd like to see it before we build.

# Camera moves

From run 021 (14-15 Nov 2026): "My Mom Cracked The Dinosaur" re-made with the boy and his mom.
Code: `~/Projects/blender-funnel/recipes/camera_moves.py` and `runs/021-reference-shots/build.py` (Mac and box copies
identical on 15 Nov; not committed). Results and lessons: `runs/021-reference-shots/review.md`.

## What run 021 found (review.md, 14 Nov)
- Driving the camera straight from the analyzer's 2D numbers: zoom curve correlation 0.86, pan 0.93, all 9 big
  zooms the right way, but 0 of 55 orbit frames: an orbit cannot come from 2D readings.
- The reference's moves curve round their subject. COLMAP solve of the opening shot put on our boy: camera round
  the boy 91.8 deg vs the reference's 93.3 deg (frames 9-48).
- Measured on 15 Nov from the saved camera paths: driven camera 14 frames fully still, buttons 0; the move test's
  first part turns +229 deg round the pair (setting 230).
- Still wrong on 14 Nov: whips (36 reference whip frames, 2 read in ours: too little blur, hard cuts), the push at
  5.87-6.7 s (+84.9 % reference vs +30.1 % ours, cause not found), the S10 pull back passing behind the boy's head.

## The order of work
1. Read the reference with skill `clip-analyzer` (scan + build_data; captions + onscreen + script_align for who the
   camera is on when each line is said).
2. Solve the real 3D path of each shot with COLMAP on the box (skill `clip-analyzer`, "Real 3D camera path").
   Short shots can't solve their own lens: fix it at the long shot's value.
3. Build the camera from buttons round OUR subject, timing and amounts taken from steps 1-2. A button never copies
   the reference's raw path into our room: our room and people are a different size and place.
4. Check (below), render on the pod, analyse the render exactly like the reference, compare (skill `learning-loop`).
5. Show Nick (below).

## The buttons (`recipes/camera_moves.py`)
Each returns one (eye, rotation, lens) per frame round a subject point. Defaults measured on the dino reference:
| button | default from the reference |
|---|---|
| `push(target, az, el, d0, d1, n, slows, aim_to)` | push in +289 % in 0.77 s (4.07 s); with d1 > d0 it is the pull out (-68 % in 0.33 s at 0.00 s) and with `aim_to` the reveal (-77 % in 1.03 s, aim slides to a second subject) |
| `orbit(target, az0, el0, d0, n, angle=92, rise=0.17, slows)` | about 92 deg round the subject, rising 17 % of its distance, over 1.3 s, slowing at 23 % and 75 % and nearly stopping at 92-100 % |
| `crane(target, az, el0, el1, d0, d1, n, aim_to)` | size -29 % with the view tilting up, camera rising (11.3 s) |
| `whip(eye, aim0, aim1, n)` | one fast eased turn, about 0.1 s |
| `hold(target, az, el, dist, n, drift)` | never fully still: a small drift |
| `orbit_flyback_arc(boy, mom, wide_eye, wide_aim, ...)` | "over" to "rope" (2.73-4.13 s): whip orbit round the pair (angle 230 default, by eye), fly back to a wide spot, arc in round Mom 33 deg closing to 0.46 (COLMAP 3.53-3.97 s) |
Helpers: `distance_for(size)` (ECU / CU / MED / WIDE to metres), `distance_for_pair(a, b)` (two people fit a 9:16
frame), `progress(n, floor, slows)` (eased, never stopped). Lens 36.1 mm (the reference's solved lens).

`check_states(scene, states, subject, see_through=CHARACTER_NAMES)` runs the four checks and fixes what it can:
1 camera inside geometry: moved in front of the hit (characters don't count: over-the-shoulder is a choice);
2 subject inside 45 % of the frame width from centre; 3 never still (1 mm or 0.05 deg a frame);
4 too close: nothing within 10 cm of the lens (counted, not fixed: change the plan).

## build.py switches (environment)
Runs on the pod with the `bpy` module (never on the Mac): see `~/funnel_021_inner6.sh` for the exact pod setup
(uv venv, requirements.txt, apt libraries, `tools/fetch_assets.sh`) and `~/limited.py` for the hard time limit.
`python runs/021-reference-shots/build.py W H SAMPLES` with:

```
report.json
"""Camera move buttons: each move is built around OUR subject and room, with timing measured from a reference.

A move returns one camera state per frame: (eye, rotation matrix, lens), the same form run 021 keys.
The camera always circles, pushes or turns relative to a subject point, so it cannot drift onto empty
walls the way copied 2D readings did (run 021, 14 Nov 2026). Defaults are measured on the reference
"My Mom Cracked The Dinosaur" (clip analyzer + COLMAP solve, run 021 review.md):
  pull_out   size -68% in 0.33 s                   (0.00 s, the boy)
  orbit      about 92 deg round the subject, rising 17% of its distance, over 1.3 s, slowing at 23%
             and 75% of the move and nearly stopping at 92-100% (0.3-1.6 s, the boy)
  push       size +289% in 0.77 s                  (4.07 s, Mom, "This is a T-Rex")
  reveal     size -77% in 1.03 s, aim slides to a second subject (8.0 s, bone to the boy)
  crane      size -29% with the view tilting up, the camera rising (11.3 s, toward the T-Rex)
  whip       one fast turn, about 0.1 s, eased     (1.9 s)
Size in frame goes as 1 / distance, so "size x k" = "distance / k".
Checks (check_states): the subject stays inside the frame, the camera never sits inside geometry (a ray
from the subject to the camera must reach it; if something is in the way the camera is moved in front
of it), and the camera is never fully still (every frame moves or turns a little).
"""
import math

from mathutils import Matrix, Vector

UP = Vector((0.0, 0.0, 1.0))
LENS = 36.1                                   # mm, vertical 36 mm sensor: the reference's solved lens
FRAME_H = {"ECU": 0.32, "CU": 0.55, "MED": 1.2, "WIDE": 3.6}   # metres of subject height the frame shows


def vfov(lens=LENS):
    return 2 * math.atan(18.0 / lens)


def distance_for(size, lens=LENS):
    """Camera distance that shows FRAME_H[size] metres top to bottom."""
    return FRAME_H[size] / (2 * math.tan(vfov(lens) / 2))


def distance_for_pair(a, b, lens=LENS, fill=0.8):
    """Distance at which two subjects a and b, side by side, fit across `fill` of the (narrow, 9:16) frame width.
    Run 021: a two-shot at a 'medium' distance put both people outside a vertical frame."""
    hfov = 2 * math.atan(18.0 * 9 / 16 / lens)
```

```
def progress(n, floor=0.15, slows=()):
    """0..1 over n frames: eased in and out, never fully stopped (floor = share of plain constant speed).
    slows: (where 0..1, how deep 0..1, how wide 0..1) dips in speed, like the reference's pauses."""
    if n <= 1:
        return [1.0] * n
    speed = []
    for k in range(n):
        u = (k + 0.5) / n
        s = (1 - floor) * 30 * u * u * (1 - u) * (1 - u) + floor          # derivative of smootherstep + floor
        for at, depth, width in slows:
            s *= 1 - depth * math.exp(-((u - at) / width) ** 2)
        speed.append(max(s, 1e-3))
    total, acc, out = sum(speed), 0.0, []
    for s in speed:
        acc += s
        out.append(acc / total)
    return out


def look(eye, target, roll=0.0):
    f = (target - eye).normalized()
    r = f.cross(UP)
    r = r.normalized() if r.length > 1e-6 else Vector((1.0, 0.0, 0.0))
    u = r.cross(f)
    if roll:
        R = Matrix.Rotation(math.radians(roll), 3, f)
        r, u = R @ r, R @ u
    return Matrix((r, u, -f)).transposed()


def around(target, az_deg, el_deg, dist):
    a, e = math.radians(az_deg), math.radians(el_deg)
    return target + Vector((math.cos(e) * math.cos(a), math.cos(e) * math.sin(a), math.sin(e))) * dist


def hold(target, az, el, dist, n, drift=0.04, lens=LENS):
    """No big move: a slow push of `drift` (share of distance) so the camera is never still."""
    p = progress(n, floor=1.0)
    return [(around(target, az, el, dist * (1 - drift * t)), look(around(target, az, el, dist * (1 - drift * t)), target), lens) for t in p]


def push(target, az, el, d0, d1, n, slows=(), lens=LENS, aim_to=None):
    """Dolly along the line to the subject from distance d0 to d1 (pull out: d1 > d0). aim_to slides the aim."""
    out = []
    for t in progress(n, slows=slows):
        aim = target.lerp(aim_to, t) if aim_to is not None else target
        eye = around(aim, az, el, d0 + (d1 - d0) * t)
        out.append((eye, look(eye, aim), lens))
    return out


def orbit(target, az0, el0, d0, n, angle=92.0, rise=0.17, d1=None, slows=((0.23, 0.6, 0.05), (0.75, 0.4, 0.05), (0.96, 0.9, 0.06)), lens=LENS):
    """Circle the subject by `angle` degrees (+ = counter-clockwise from above), rising by `rise` x distance."""
    d1 = d0 if d1 is None else d1
    out = []
    for t in progress(n, slows=slows):
        d = d0 + (d1 - d0) * t
        el = math.degrees(math.asin(max(-0.95, min(0.95, math.sin(math.radians(el0)) + rise * t))))
        eye = around(target, az0 + angle * t, el, d)
        out.append((eye, look(eye, target), lens))
    return out


def crane(target, az, el0, el1, d0, d1, n, aim_to=None, lens=LENS):
    """Rise from elevation el0 to el1 while moving from d0 to d1; aim_to tilts the view toward a reveal."""
    out = []
    for t in progress(n):
        aim = target.lerp(aim_to, t) if aim_to is not None else target
        eye = around(target, az, el0 + (el1 - el0) * t, d0 + (d1 - d0) * t)
        out.append((eye, look(eye, aim), lens))
    return out


def whip(eye, aim0, aim1, n, lens=LENS):
    """One fast turn in place from aim0 to aim1 (blur comes from the render's motion blur)."""
    out = []
    d0, d1 = (aim0 - eye).normalized(), (aim1 - eye).normalized()
    for t in progress(n, floor=0.05):
        d = d0.slerp(d1, t) if d0.angle(d1) > 1e-4 else d0
        out.append((eye.copy(), look(eye, eye + d), lens))
    return out
```

- tool `Write`: `{"file_path": "/Users/nick/Projects/blender-funnel/tools/make_zoom_presets.py", "content": "\"\"\"Build the zoom preset table from the clip analyzer's reading of a reference clip.\n\nA \"zoom\" here = a move the analyzer reads with a size change of 20% or more (run 021 review.md uses\nthe same cut: 9 zooms in the first 12.6 s of \"My Mom Cracked The Dinosaur\").\nEvery number is computed from the per-frame zoom series (% size per second), nothing is typed in.\n\nUsage: python tools/make_zoom_presets.py runs/021-reference-shots/motion_0_12.json recipes/zoom_presets.json\n\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport math\nimport sys\n\nMIN_ZOOM = 20.0       # % size change that counts as a zoom\nPOINTS = 9            # speed profile resolution\n\n\ndef build(motion):\n    fps = motion[\"fps\"]\n    z = motion[\"series\"][\"zoom_per_s\"]\n    presets = []\n    for mv in motion[\"moves\"]:\n        if abs(mv[\"zoom_total\"]) < MIN_ZOOM:\n            continue\n        seg = z[mv[\"a\"]:mv[\"b\"] + 1]\n        size, logs = 1.0, []\n        for v in seg:\n            size *= 1 + v / 100 / fps\n            logs.append(math.log(size))\n        total = (size - 1) * 100\n        assert abs(total - mv[\"zoom_total\"]) < 0.2, (mv, total)       # recomputed = analyzer's own total\n        end = logs[-1]\n        profile = [logs[min(len(logs) - 1, round(k * (len(logs) - 1) / (POINTS - 1)))] / end for k in range(POINTS)]\n        profile[-1] = 1.0\n        presets.append({\n            \"id\": f\"Z{len(presets) + 1}\",\n            \"label\": mv[\"label\"],\n            \"start_s\": mv[\"start\"],\n            \"dur_s\": mv[\"dur\"],\n            \"frames\": len(seg),\n            \"size_pct\": round(total, 1),\n            \"profile\": [round(p, 3) for p in profile],     # share of the (log) size change done at each ninth\n            \"pan_pct\": mv[\"pan\"],                           # stored from the analyzer, NOT applied by zoom_states\n            \"tilt_pct\": mv[\"tilt\"],                         # stored from the analyzer, NOT applied by zoom_states\n        })\n    return {\"source\": motion[\"source\"], \"fps\": fps, \"min_zoom_pct\": MIN_ZOOM, \"presets\": presets}\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    data = build(json.load(open(sys.argv[1])))\n    json.dump(data, open(sys.argv[2], \"w\"), indent=1)\n    print(len(data[\"presets\"]), \"presets ->\", sys.argv[2])\n"}`
- tool `Write`: `{"file_path": "/Users/nick/Projects/blender-funnel/recipes/zoom_presets.py", "content": "\"\"\"Zoom presets: one per big zoom (size change of 20% or more) the clip analyzer read in the reference.\n\nTable = recipes/zoom_presets.json, made by tools/make_zoom_presets.py from the analyzer's per-frame\nnumbers. Each preset has a size change (%), a duration and a speed profile (how the size change is\nspread over the move). `zoom_curve` is pure maths; `zoom_states` turns it into camera states with\nrecipes/camera_moves (needs bpy's mathutils, so it runs on the pod, not on the Mac).\n\nDials for the game: `strength` scales the size change (1.0 = the reference's), `dur_scale` stretches time.\nSize in frame goes as 1 / distance, so a +289% zoom means distance / 3.89.\npan_pct and tilt_pct are stored in the table for later; they are not applied here.\n\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport math\nimport os\n\nTABLE = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), \"zoom_presets.json\")\n\n\ndef load():\n    return {p[\"id\"]: p for p in json.load(open(TABLE))[\"presets\"]}\n\n\ndef frames_for(preset, fps=30.03, dur_scale=1.0):\n    return max(2, round(preset[\"dur_s\"] * dur_scale * fps))\n\n\ndef zoom_curve(preset, n, strength=1.0):\n    \"\"\"Size factor at each of n frames (1.0 at the start, ends at 1 + size_pct/100 for strength 1).\"\"\"\n    total = math.log(1 + preset[\"size_pct\"] / 100) * strength\n    prof = preset[\"profile\"]\n    out = []\n    for k in range(n):\n        u = k / (n - 1) * (len(prof) - 1)\n        i = min(int(u), len(prof) - 2)\n        p = prof[i] + (prof[i + 1] - prof[i]) * (u - i)\n        out.append(math.exp(total * p))\n    return out\n\n\ndef zoom_states(preset, target, az, el, d_start, n=None, strength=1.0, dur_scale=1.0, aim_to=None):\n    \"\"\"Camera states (eye, rotation, lens) for one zoom: the camera dollies along the line to `target`.\"\"\"\n    import camera_moves as cm  # same folder; imported late because mathutils exists only inside Blender\n    n = n or frames_for(preset, dur_scale=dur_scale)\n    out = []\n    for t, size in zip([k / (n - 1) for k in range(n)], zoom_curve(preset, n, strength)):\n        aim = target.lerp(aim_to, t) if aim_to is not None else target\n        eye = cm.around(aim, az, el, d_start / size)\n        out.append((eye, cm.look(eye, aim), cm.LENS))\n    return out\n"}`

```
    ("S10 bone close, dolly out", 226, TOE + (BH - TOE).normalized() * 0.5, TOE, 30.0),
    ("S11 crack close", 280, TOE + Vector((-0.15, -0.5, 0.2)), TOE, 40.0),
    ("S12 boy close-up from below", 302, BH + FB * 0.55 + Vector((0, 0, -0.35)), BH, 30.0),
    ("S13 boy's back, out to the wide", 329, BH - FB * 0.6 + Vector((0, 0, 0.02)), BH + FB, 28.0),
]

M = json.load(open(os.path.join(HERE, "motion_0_12.json")))
FPS, N = M["fps"], M["n"]
ST, LAB, SER = M["status"], M["label"], M["series"]


def smoothed(values, weights=(1, 2, 3, 2, 1)):
    """5-frame weighted average inside each run of tracked frames (never across a whip, cut or lost frame).

    The analyzer's per-frame readings carry measurement noise; driven straight into the camera it
    became real shake (run 021 v1: twice the reference's pan reversals). Measured on 0-12.6 s: this
    cuts the frame-to-frame change (jerk) of zoom, pan and tilt several times.
    The running total (where the camera has got to) is what gets smoothed, with each run's first and
    last value pinned, so every run still adds up to exactly the same move."""
    import numpy as np
    x, w, k = np.array(values, float), np.array(weights, float), len(weights) // 2
    w = w / w.sum()
    y, a = x.copy(), None
    for i in range(len(x) + 1):
        tracked = i < len(x) and ST[i] == "track"
        if tracked and a is None:
            a = i
        if not tracked and a is not None:
            c = np.concatenate([[0.0], np.cumsum(x[a:i])])
            cs = np.convolve(np.pad(c, k, mode="edge"), w, "valid")
            cs[0], cs[-1] = c[0], c[-1]
            y[a:i] = np.diff(cs)
            a = None
```

```
---
name: pod-guard-before-every-run
description: "RunPod workbench hands out slow cards and pods without the HumGen drive; check the card, the drive and put a time limit on every run"
metadata:
  node_type: memory
  type: feedback
  originSessionId: 790abea7-31fc-4731-b1fb-ea1c5aa4cbdc
  modified: 2026-11-11T16:46:40.374Z
---

On 2026-11-11 (king head and outfit session, faces/king18) Nick asked: "how do we make sure we never get stuck on slow pods? this costs money and time."

What happened that day, measured first-hand:
- `~/Desktop/render-desk/bin/workbench up` takes whatever card is free. 4090 pods rendered five 256-sample close ups in about a minute; RTX 4000 Ada pods needed 3 minutes for one 16-sample close up of the same scene (roughly ten times slower, pod CPU included). A run sat 14 minutes with no output before I noticed.
- Three of four pods in one stretch started WITHOUT the network volume, so without HumGen (render desk falls back to "no volume: this data centre was empty").
- A `workbench up` that is interrupted leaves a half set up pod (no rsync); it needs down and up again.

**Why:** dead starts and stalled runs cost about 0.3 USD and well over half an hour that day, and Nick watches both.

**How to apply:** right after `workbench up`, read the card name in its last line and, when the job needs HumGen, run `faces/king18/probe_pod.py` (a few seconds). Wrap every `pod_run.py` call in a hard time limit (a small subprocess wrapper that kills the process group; macOS has no `timeout`). On a slow card either cut samples and shots or say so and wait, never just let it run. Never chain long runs in one foreground command; use a background script. The lasting fix (allowed-card list, max minutes per run, refuse a pod without the volume) belongs in render desk and `tools/pod_run.py`, which are shared tools: it needs Nick's go (asked 2026-11-11, not yet answered).

RENDERING IS OFF LIMITS unless Nick explicitly says to render. Full renders make his machine really slow and eat his time and credits.

**Why:** On 2026-08-05 I ran ~10 full scene renders (each 2 to 10+ minutes) iterating on clouds and birds by guesswork. A volumetric render was still chewing the CPU after 10 minutes while he was trying to work. His words: "rendering is off limits unless i say so. you make my machine really slow."

**How to apply:**
- Default loop = RESEARCH first (see [[research-before-execute]]), then build the .blend, then show a **viewport snapshot** via the BlenderMCP bridge (`get_viewport_screenshot`), which is nearly free. Open the GUI with `blender <file>.blend` and screenshot it.
- Build scripts should SAVE the .blend and stop. Do not chain a render onto the end by default; gate rendering behind an env var or an explicit ask.
- If a render is genuinely needed, ask first and say how long it w ...[cut]
```

```
#!/bin/sh
# funnel_021_own.sh: run 021 v2 re-render (Nick: "Yes, re-render", 14 Nov 2026). Uses a pod that is already up
# (then leaves it up); otherwise rents one and takes it down at the end. Hard cap 30 minutes.
WB=/Users/nick/Desktop/render-desk/bin/workbench; export WB_NO_BLENDER=1
LOG=/Users/nick/funnel_021_mt.log
REPO=/Users/nick/run021mt-repo
T0=$(date +%s)
{
  date +%H:%M:%S
  MINE=0
  if "$WB" status 2>&1 | tail -1 | grep -q "WB up"; then echo "using the pod that is already up; not taking it down"; else MINE=1; python3 /Users/nick/limited.py 540 "$WB" up; echo "UPRC=$?"; fi
  if "$WB" status 2>&1 | tail -1 | grep -q "WB up"; then
    "$WB" status 2>&1 | tail -1
    mkdir -p "$REPO/runs"
    rsync -a --exclude .git --exclude assets --exclude 'runs/*' /Users/nick/Projects/blender-funnel/ "$REPO/"
    rsync -a --exclude out --exclude "out_*" --exclude out_test --exclude review --exclude __pycache__ /Users/nick/Projects/blender-funnel/runs/021-reference-shots "$REPO/runs/"
    "$WB" push "$REPO"; echo "PUSH_REPO_RC=$?"
    "$WB" push /Users/nick/funnel_021_inner6.sh; echo "PUSH_SCRIPT_RC=$?"
    "$WB" push /Users/nick/limited.py; echo "PUSH_LIMIT_RC=$?"
    LEFT=$((1740 - ($(date +%s) - T0)))
    echo "run limit: $LEFT s"
    if [ "$LEFT" -gt 400 ]; then
      python3 /Users/nick/limited.py "$LEFT" "$WB" run "bash /Users/nick/funnel_021_inner6.sh $((LEFT - 60))"; echo "RUN_RC=$?"
      for v in A B C; do mkdir -p "$REPO/runs/021-reference-shots/out_mt_$v"; "$WB" pull "$REPO/runs/021-reference-shots/out_mt_$v/" "$REPO/runs/021-reference-shots/out_mt_$v/"; echo "PULL_$v=$?"; done
    else echo "RUN_SKIPPED: pod took too long"; fi
  else echo "NO_POD: could not get a pod"; fi
  if [ "$MINE" = 1 ]; then "$WB" down 2>&1 | tail -2; fi
  "$WB" status 2>&1 | tail -1
  echo "time used: $(( $(date +%s) - T0 )) s"
  ls "$REPO/runs/021-reference-shots/out_mt_C/frames" 2>/dev/null | wc -l
  date +%H:%M:%S; echo FINISHED
} > "$LOG" 2>&1
```

```
#!/bin/bash
# funnel_021_inner6.sh: runs ON THE POD. Run 021 move test ("over" to "rope", frames 82-124), three versions.
set -u
START=$(date +%s)
LIMIT=${1:-1200}
R=/Users/nick/run021mt-repo
t() { echo; echo "### [$(date +%H:%M:%S)] $*"; }
t "setup"
W=/root/funnel; mkdir -p "$W"
export UV_CACHE_DIR="$W/uv-cache" UV_PYTHON_INSTALL_DIR="$W/python" UV_LINK_MODE=copy
python3 -m pip install -q uv 2>&1 | grep -v notice | tail -2
python3 -m uv venv -q --python 3.13 "$W/venv" 2>&1 | tail -2
PY="$W/venv/bin/python"
python3 -m uv pip install -q --python "$PY" -r "$R/requirements.txt" 2>&1 | tail -3
apt-get update -qq >/dev/null 2>&1
DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive apt-get install -y -qq libxrender1 libxi6 libxkbcommon0 libsm6 libice6 libgl1 libegl1 libxfixes3 libxxf86vm1 libgomp1 libx11-6 libxext6 >/dev/null 2>&1
"$PY" -c "import bpy; print('bpy', bpy.app.version_string)" 2>&1 | tail -1
cd "$R"
[ -e assets ] || bash tools/fetch_assets.sh 2>&1 | tail -1
for V in "A 230 1.3 -12 0.5" "B 270 1.3 -12 0.5" "C 270 1.0 -18 1.0"; do
  set -- $V
  LEFT=$((LIMIT - $(date +%s) + START))
  [ "$LEFT" -lt 120 ] && { echo "LIMIT: skipping version $1"; continue; }
  t "version $1: angle $2, distance $3, height $4, blur $5 (limit ${LEFT}s)"
  ( time MODE=anim FRAMES=82-124 MOVETEST=1 MT_ANGLE=$2 MT_D=$3 MT_EL=$4 MT_SHUTTER=$5 DEVICE=gpu HAIR=30000 OUT=$R/runs/021-reference-shots/out_mt_$1 \
      python3 /Users/nick/limited.py "$LEFT" "$PY" runs/021-reference-shots/build.py 360 640 16 ) 2>&1 | grep "MOVETEST\|real\|rror" | tail -8
  ls runs/021-reference-shots/out_mt_$1/frames | wc -l
done
t "done"
```

### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T10:59

The nine zoom presets are built and the pod render is running. It will tell me when it finishes, and I'll then score it against the reference and send you the results.

**What exists now:**
- **Zoom presets:** `recipes/zoom_presets.json` has 9 presets, one for each move the analyzer reads at 20% or more (the same 9 as in run 021's review). Each stores its size change, duration and a 9-point speed profile, all computed from the analyzer's per-frame numbers.
- **Checked:** the table was recomputed and matches the analyzer's own totals. Each preset's curve starts at 1.0 and ends at the measured size.
- **The run:** `ZOOMS=1` in `build.py` now uses the presets for all nine zooms. It also adds plans for shots 12 and 13, so zooms 8 and 9 (at 10.97 s and 11.33 s) are covered. The render covers the whole clip, 379 frames, with a no-render path check first.

**What is not done or not verified:**
- **Pan and tilt:** they are stored in the table but not applied. Zoom 8 ("move left", -61% pan) will therefore come out as a plain pull-out.
- **Shot 12:** I haven't matched the reference's small orbit there. It is a held shot with a slow drift.
- **Orbits and whips:** they are untouched. Only the zoom presets are new.
- **Not run yet:** none of this has been run yet, so I can't say it works.
- **Nothing committed.**

```
"""Run 021 check: our render vs the reference, by eye (side-by-side video) and by number (clip analyzer).

1. frames -> ours.mp4 at the reference's frame rate, side_by_side.mp4 (reference 0-12.62 s left, ours right, reference sound)
2. clip analyzer (scan.py + build_data.py, same caption band as the reference) on ours -> clips/run021-ours
3. for every reference move: the zoom and pan it adds up to in the reference vs in ours over the same frames
Usage: python3 compare.py FRAMES_DIR
"""
import json
import math
import os
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys

FR = sys.argv[1]
HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
REF = "/Users/nick/Desktop/My Mom Cracked The Dinosaur 🦖.mp4"
CA = "/Users/nick/Projects/clip-analyzer"
OUT = os.path.join(CA, "clips", "run021-ours")
FPS = 30.0
run = lambda *a: subprocess.run(list(a), check=True)

ours = os.path.join(HERE, "ours.mp4")
run("ffmpeg", "-v", "error", "-y", "-framerate", str(FPS), "-start_number", "1", "-i", os.path.join(FR, "f%04d.png"),
    "-c:v", "libx264", "-pix_fmt", "yuv420p", "-crf", "16", ours)
run("ffmpeg", "-v", "error", "-y", "-t", "12.62", "-i", REF, "-i", ours, "-filter_complex",
    "[0:v]scale=360:640,setsar=1[a];[1:v]scale=360:640,setsar=1[b];[a][b]hstack=inputs=2[v]",
    "-map", "[v]", "-map", "0:a?", "-c:v", "libx264", "-pix_fmt", "yuv420p", "-crf", "18", "-c:a", "aac", "-shortest",
    os.path.join(HERE, "side_by_side.mp4"))

os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
shutil.copy(ours, os.path.join(OUT, "clip.mp4"))
run("python3", "-I", f"{CA}/scan.py", os.path.join(OUT, "clip.mp4"), OUT, "--band", "0.715,0.785,0.08,0.92")
run("python3", "-I", f"{CA}/build_data.py", OUT)

R = json.load(open(f"{CA}/clips/dino/data.json"))
O = json.load(open(f"{OUT}/data.json"))
n = min(O["n"], 379)


def totals(D, a, b):
    s = D["series"]
    z = 1.0
    px = py = 0.0
    for i in range(a, min(b, n - 1) + 1):
        if D["status"][i] in ("cut", "lost"):
            continue
        if D["status"][i] == "track":
            z *= 1 + (s["zoom_per_s"][i] or 0) / 100 / FPS
        px += (s["pan_x_per_s"][i] or 0) / FPS
        py += (s["pan_y_per_s"][i] or 0) / FPS
    return round((z - 1) * 100, 1), round(px, 1), round(py, 1)


rows = []
for m in R["moves"]:
    if m["a"] >= n or m["label"] == "cut":
        continue
    rz, rx, ry = totals(R, m["a"], m["b"])
    oz, ox, oy = totals(O, m["a"], m["b"])
    olab = max(set(O["label"][m["a"]:m["b"] + 1]), key=O["label"][m["a"]:m["b"] + 1].count)
    rows.append(dict(t=m["start"], dur=m["dur"], ref_label=m["label"], our_label=olab,
                     ref_zoom=rz, our_zoom=oz, ref_pan=rx, our_pan=ox, ref_tilt=ry, our_tilt=oy))
big = [r for r in rows if abs(r["ref_zoom"]) >= 20]
same_dir = sum(1 for r in big if r["ref_zoom"] * r["our_zoom"] > 0)
whips_r = sum(1 for r in rows if r["ref_label"].startswith("whip"))
whips_o = sum(1 for r in rows if r["ref_label"].startswith("whip") and r["our_label"].startswith("whip"))
corr = lambda a, b: (sum((x - sum(a) / len(a)) * (y - sum(b) / len(b)) for x, y in zip(a, b)) /
                     math.sqrt(sum((x - sum(a) / len(a)) ** 2 for x in a) * sum((y - sum(b) / len(b)) ** 2 for y in b) + 1e-12))
zr = [R["series"]["zoom_per_s"][i] or 0 for i in range(n)]
zo = [O["series"]["zoom_per_s"][i] or 0 for i in range(n)]
pr = [R["series"]["pan_x_per_s"][i] or 0 for i in range(n)]
po = [O["series"]["pan_x_per_s"][i] or 0 for i in range(n)]
summary = dict(frames=n, big_zoom_moves=len(big), same_direction=same_dir, ref_whips=whips_r, whips_read_as_whips_in_ours=whips_o,
               zoom_curve_correlation=round(corr(zr, zo), 2), pan_curve_correlation=round(corr(pr, po), 2))
json.dump(dict(summary=summary, moves=rows), open(os.path.join(HERE, "compare.json"), "w"), indent=1)
print("SUMMARY", json.dumps(summary))
for r in big:
    print(f"{r['t']:5.2f}s {r['ref_label']:<12} ref zoom {r['ref_zoom']:+7.1f}%  ours {r['our_zoom']:+7.1f}%  ({r['our_label']})")

run0 ...[cut]
```

|---|---|---|---|
| 0.00 s | pull out | -68.1% | -74.4% |
| 1.63 s | push in | +35.4% | +11.9% |
| 2.37 s | pull out | -20.1% | -12.5% |

- **Overall match:** the zoom correlation is **0.61** and the pan correlation is **0.06**, against 0.86 and 0.93 for the earlier run that copied the analyzer's raw numbers. The presets only change size, and nothing pans.
- **Per zoom, closest:** at 6.13 s we got +51.0% against +48.1%. The other near ones are 0.00 s and 4.07 s.
- **Per zoom, furthest:** the 1.63 s push (+11.9% against +35.4%), and the 10.97 s and 11.33 s zooms (-18.6% and -11.8% against about -29%).
- **Not fixed:**
  - Whips: only 2 of the 36 reference whips read as whips in ours.
  - Shot 12: the path check flags it "too close" for all 26 frames. It looks fine in the pictures, but the check isn't resolved.
  - Shot 3: the same check flags 9 frames.
- **Cost:** $0.09, and the pod is down.

**Files:**
- The table is `recipes/zoom_presets.json`.
- The switch is `ZOOMS=1` in `build.py`.
- The comparison and numbers are in `review/zooms/` (`compare.json`).

Nothing is committed.

### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T11:17

Your eye is right, and the numbers back it. I measured the frame-to-frame change on both videos, and the chart above shows it.

**What I measured, from the two videos:**
- **Cuts:** ours has 12 hard jumps, one at each shot change. 11 of them line up exactly with shot starts at frames 60, 69, 97, 102, 120, 167, 176, 200, 281, 303 and 329. The 12th jump is the whip out of shot 1 at frame 56. The reference has no isolated hard jumps like these. Its jumps come in clusters, which are the whips smearing from one shot into the next.
- **Energy:** the median change per frame is 11.6 in the reference and 1.44 in ours. Ours is calm and static between cuts, while the reference never settles.
- **Also hard in ours:** frames 256 to 259, around 8.5 s, where the review already noted the boy's hair fills the frame.

**What is wrong in our process:**
- **Hand-built shots:** each of the 13 shots is a separate camera setup with its own start, so every boundary is a hard cut.
- **Scoring:** we score the size of each zoom, which is why the table looks fine while the video doesn't. Nothing scores cuts or smoothness.
- **Presets:** they only do size.
- **Whips:** we only render 2 of the 36 reference whips.

**The fix I'd make, in order:**
1. Add a smoothness and cut-count score to the comparison, so the checker catches this and you don't have to.
2. Turn every shot change into a whip with motion blur, using the reference's whip timings.
3. Add pan and tilt to the presets.

🔴 **NEEDED:** may I do these three in that order? **My pick is yes.** The first two re-render the clip again for about $0.09 each.

### USER 2026-11-15T11:22

from what you did, what do you think is the biggest weakness in our system?


### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T11:22

**Biggest weakness: our checker doesn't measure what you feel, so it called a bad video good.**

- **What happened:** the zoom table looked fine (all 9 zooms the right way), while the picture-change correlation with the reference was 0.05. We only found the spikes-then-nothing problem because you saw it. Nothing in the process would have caught it.
- **Why it keeps happening:** we score each move's size and pan totals, not the feel of the clip (cuts, frame-to-frame energy, whips). Every earlier fix was tuned against that blind score, so we kept improving numbers you don't see.
- **The second weakness, not yet verified:** the reference's energy may come partly from people moving, with whips following them. Ours stand still, so the camera alone may never reach the same energy. That idea comes from a code comment in `build.py` quoting an earlier remark of yours, and I haven't tested it myself.

**My fix is the one already running:** the smoothness scorer now sits in the loop, so every variant is judged on feel first. I'll report whether matching the reference's raw whips closes the gap once the two variants finish.

### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T11:29

**Matching the reference did not work well enough.** Pod cost was $0.11, and the pod is down.

| render | median change | quiet-frame floor (5th percentile) | match with the reference's rhythm (1/3 s) |
|---|---|---|---|
| reference | 11.6 | 2.95 | 1.00 |
| earlier driven render | 7.0 | not measured | 0.61 |
| match A (raw whips, no smoothing) | 6.7 | 0.82 | 0.55 |
| match B (A plus longer whip blur) | 6.7 | not measured | 0.55 |
| zoom presets | 1.44 | 0.43 | 0.05 |

- **Where the energy is missing:** match A is about 12 to 15% the reference's energy... precisely, it falls short in 0 to 2 s (4.7 against 11.9), 2 to 4 s (13 to 15 against 23 to 24), 7 to 8 s (5.5 against 12) and 10 to 12 s (8 to 9 against 14 to 15). It matches in 4 to 7 s and 9 to 10 s.
- **The extra whip blur barely changed the scores:** A and B are different images, but score the same to two decimals.
- **A new finding:** even the reference's quietest frames change by about 3, and ours by under 1. Since the characters barely move, the reference's camera never really rests. I can't tell yet whether that is handheld shake or video noise, so it is **not verified**.

**Now your fallback, my way:** keep the zoom presets and add two things to them:
- a constant small handheld drift, sized from the reference's quiet-frame floor;
- a whip with motion blur at each of the 12 shot changes.

🔴 **NEEDED:** go ahead with that and one more pod run? **My pick is yes**, about $0.10.

### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T11:32

Looking at the frames themselves, the problem is not the numbers: **the reference does one continuous move there, and ours is a chain of unrelated framings.**

**What I see in frames 82 to 124 (2.7 to 4.1 s):**
- **Reference:**
  - From frame 82 to 100 the camera swings round the boy and Mom, so both stay in frame while the blur streaks past.
  - From frame 103 the camera is low under the dinosaur's leg and slowly arcs in toward the two of them.
  - It is one smooth path, and both people stay in the picture the whole time.
- **Ours (matching attempt):**
  - Frames 82 to 94: the boy on the left with Mom's hair at the edge.
  - Frame 97: a sudden wide room shot.
  - Frames 103 to 115: a big grey slab fills the lower half, with Mom hidden behind it.
  - Frames 118 to 124: Mom only, in the foreground.
  - Each change of framing reads as a jump, then the camera drifts and corrects, and that is the zig-zag you feel.

**Why the process produces this:** we build shot by shot, from hand-set cameras and 2D readings. A move that circles both people can't come from that. The real 3D path (the COLMAP solve) is how we see what the camera truly does. It exists for shots 3 and 5 to 11, but only 7 of 28 frames are solved for shot 3 and 2 of 26 for shot 9. The `orbit_flyback_arc` button for exactly this stretch was written, but its 3 test renders were never looked at.

**My plan, judged by eye on frame sheets like this one and not by scores:**
1. Render those 3 orbit versions for 2.7 to 4.1 s and compare them with the reference sheet.
2. Keep the best version and repeat the same way, stretch by stretch.

🔴 **NEEDED:** go ahead with step 1? **My pick is yes.** It is one short pod run, about $0.10, and I'll show you the sheets and wait for your verdict.

# Clip analyzer

Built with Nick on 14-15 Nov 2026 to study "My Mom Cracked The Dinosaur" for the run 021 Blender remake.
Everything runs on the Mac except the COLMAP solve (box). Blender is never involved here.
Every command below was run on 15 Nov 2026 and worked (numbers in brackets are what it printed that day).

## The page
- Start: preview_start name `clip-analyzer` (launch.json), or `PORT=8570 python3 ~/Projects/clip-analyzer/server.py`.
- Upload a clip on the page: it runs voice split, sound, captions, scan, build_data by itself.
- The player shows the clip with the label of every frame, move list, arrows (flow), sound on/off and stems.
- Verdict buttons save to `clips/<id>/feedback.jsonl` (`{"clip","start","label","verdict","correct"}`).
  Nothing tunes from them yet: read them and turn each one into a check (skill `learning-loop`).
- Off switches: `CLIP_UPLOAD=0` refuses uploads, `CLIP_SOUND=0` skips voice split, sound and captions.
- Gate: `./verify.sh` (synthetic 3D moves, upload, off switch, sound, captions) ["all checks passed"]. Never edit
  verify.sh, checks/ or program.md.

## Run it by hand on a new clip
Make `clips/<id>/clip.mp4` (the file must be called clip), then, from ~/Projects/clip-analyzer:

| step | command | writes | what it means |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 motion | `python3 scan.py clips/<id>/clip.mp4 clips/<id> [--band T,B,L,R] [--no-mask]` | scan.csv, flow.json | per frame: zoom, pan, tilt, roll, whip, cut, 3D travel and parallax, light. Band = caption strip to ignore, default 0.70,0.80 of height [3 s slice: 90 frames, track 77 %, whip 18 %, lost 6 %] |
| 2 moves | `python3 build_data.py clips/<id>` | data.json | smoothed label per frame, list of moves, whips, summary (the page loads this) |
| 3 faces | `python3 onscreen.py clips/<id> [--every N]` | onscreen.json | per frame: main character (largest face), every face box, shot size ECU / CU / MED / WIDE. Characters are numbered by screen time, not named |
| 4 names | write `clips/<id>/names.json` `{"1": "mom", "2": "boy"}` | names.json | who each character number is. Done by eye or by vote against an answer key (step 8) |
| 5 captions | `python3 captions.py clips/<id> [--band T,B,L,R]` | captions.json | each burned-in caption: start, length, words (Apple's text reader via ocr.js) [8 captions on the slice] |
| 6 voice split | `~/.venvs/demucs/bin/demucs --two-stems vocals -n htdemucs -d cpu -o <work> <mix.wav>` then ffmpeg to `clips/<id>/stems/no_vocals.m4a` | stems/ | lets sound.py hear effects without the voice. The page does this on upload |
| 7 sound | `python3 sound.py clips/<id> [FPS]` | sound.json, spectrogram.png | whoosh / crack / thud / hit with times, loudness per frame |
| 8 words vs camera | `python3 script_align.py clips/<id>` (`LAG=0.25` s default) | script.json + printed checks | per caption: who is on screen, how big, which move, cut or whip near it; each rule against chance |
| 9 score faces | `python3 checks_onscreen_score.py clips/<id>` | printed | onscreen.json against `onscreen_truth.txt` (by-eye answer key) |

`label_sound.py` (AudioSet tagger, demucs venv) exists but heard nothing useful on the dino clip; it is not in the pipeline.

## How far to trust each reading (measured on the dino clip, 15 Nov 2026)
- Who is on screen: right 142 of 175 answer-key frames (81 %). Shot size: exact 56 %, within one step 95 %.
  Misses are mostly "face there, detector saw none" (mom 11, boy 7).
- Shot-size cut-offs (ECU >= 0.40, CU 0.20-0.40, MED 0.09-0.20 of frame height) were fitted on this one clip:
  confirm on a second clip before trusting them.
- Words vs camera on the dino clip: quoted lines are close-ups 73 % of 44 captions vs 41 % by chance; "mom" words
  show mom 61 % vs 46 %; prop words show no face or wide 71 % vs 38 %.
- 3D labels (dolly, truck, orbit, lens zoom) are proven on synthetic clips (verify.sh). On real footage they flicker:
  use them as hints, and use the COLMAP solve for any real 3D camera path.
- Copying 2D readings straight into a Blender camera gave a flat, jittery camera (run 021). Don't.

## Real 3D camera path: COLMAP on the box
Scripts in `tools/camera_solve/` (README there). pycolmap 4.2.1 + OpenCV in the box venv `/Users/nick/venvs/colmap`
on root@37.27.148.115. Run as nick, memory-capped, never on the Mac:

```
# frames at 540x960 as f0000.png... in DIR/images, a mask per frame DIR/masks/f0000.png.png (white = use, caption band black)
systemd-run --uid=nick --gid=nick -p MemoryMax=2G -p WorkingDirectory=WORKDIR --unit=solve-X \
  /bin/sh -c "/Users/nick/venvs/colmap/bin/python solve_shot_free_lens.py DIR > solve.log 2>&1"
```
- `solve_shot_free_lens.py DIR`: one shot, lens solved. Prints frames solved, reprojection error, and for frames 20+
  the orbit angle round the point every view looks at. Writes DIR/path.json (camera centre and rotation per frame).
  The 55-frame and frame-20 numbers are the dino opening shot's: change them for another shot.
- `solve_all_shots.py WORK VIDEO`: every shot with the lens fixed (961.4 px at 540x960, the dino opening shot's).
  Shot frame ranges are hard-coded for the dino clip: take a new clip's cuts from data.json first.
- `solve_masked_people.py`: people masked too. Failed on dino 1.9-4.1 s (blur, 55 % masked).
- `orbit_round_subject.py DIR`: how far the camera goes round the subject (points on the subject's hair).
- Compare scripts (`compare_*.py`, `score_move_versions.py`) still hold run 021 paths: fix paths before reuse.
- Dino results (14 Nov): opening shot S1 55 of 55 frames, 0.79 px, lens 36.1 mm; camera round the boy over frames
  9-48 = 93.3 deg (ours later 91.8 deg). Shots S2-S11 (runs/021-reference-shots/solved/report.json): 8 of 10 got
  some frames solved, from 2 of 26 (S9) to 55 of 55 (S10); S2 (9 frames) and S4 (5 frames) did not solve.
  Results and lessons: ~/Projects/blender-funnel/runs/021-reference-shots/review.md.
- Rerun 15 Nov (proof of these commands): solve_shot_free_lens.py on frames 0-54 gave 55 of 55, 0.796 px again
  (box, 6 min, 374 MB peak). orbit_round_subject.py on it printed fixed ranges (frames 9-54: +57.8 deg round the boy);
  its per-3-frame steps add to about 96 deg over frames 9-48. The script as saved does not print 9-48, so the
  93.3 deg is not reproduced exactly. COLMAP runs can differ a little: report the run you made, with its date.

### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T11:34

None of the three orbit versions is close to the reference, judging by eye on the frames and by the analyzer's readings on our videos. The three renders were already on disk from 11:08 to 11:10 today, so nothing new was rendered. The pod run I tried was refused because today's workbench cap of $15.00 is used up, and I did not touch the cap.

**What I see (frames 82 to 124, every 3rd frame):**
- **Reference:**
  - Both people stay in frame from frame 91 on.
  - The camera stays wide and low under the dinosaur's leg.
  - It only closes in on Mom near the end, at frame 124.
- **All three versions (A, B, C):**
  - They start on the boy at frame 82, then lose him (an empty wall at frame 88).
  - They pass Mom's face at frames 94 to 100.
  - From frame 109 on, Mom's hair fills the whole frame and the boy is gone.
  - The "arc in" runs far too close and ends on her back.
  - The three versions differ only a little from each other.
- **Direction:** the analyzer reads the reference's whips there as mostly right (11 frames) and ours mostly left or up.
- **Cuts:** it reads 4 "cut" frames inside our supposedly continuous move (A at 102, 104, 109, 113; B at 91, 92, 103, 109; C at 90 to 93). The reference has 1, at frame 101. Those jumps are the cut-and-distortion feel.

**Not done:**
- **COLMAP solve on our render:** I haven't run it. On the reference, the blurred frames of this stretch didn't solve, so ours would likely solve only the sharp frames.
- **The fix:** the orbit still has to be rebuilt.

**My proposal:** rebuild it with both people held in frame, a wide distance, and the arc stopping at a medium close-up of Mom. Do it by eye on frame sheets, with no pod needed until the sheet looks right.

```
def _rsync(pods, st, src, dst, up=True, excludes=()):
    host, port, user = st["target"]
    ssh = f"ssh -i {shlex.quote(str(pods.key))} -p {port} -o StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new -o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null -o LogLevel=ERROR"
    args = ["rsync", "-az", "-e", ssh, f"--rsync-path=touch {LAST_USE} 2>/dev/null; rsync"] + [f"--exclude={e}" for e in excludes]
    pair = [src, f"{user}@{host}:{dst}"] if up else [f"{user}@{host}:{src}", dst]
    return subprocess.run(args + pair).returncode
```

```
def _wb_left(cfg):
    """What is left of today's workbench cap (the GPU time for building a clip, finals excluded)."""
    since = dt.datetime.now().replace(hour=0, minute=0, second=0, microsecond=0); spent = 0.0
    for row in ledger.rows(cfg):
        try:
            if row.get("job") == "workbench" and dt.datetime.fromisoformat(row["when"]) >= since: spent += float(row.get("cost_usd") or 0)
        except (ValueError, KeyError):
            continue
    return float(cfg.get("WORKBENCH_DAY_CAP_USD", 6)) - spent


def _caps_left(cfg):
    now = dt.datetime.now()
    day = ledger.spent_since(cfg, now.replace(hour=0, minute=0, second=0, microsecond=0))
    month = ledger.spent_since(cfg, now.replace(day=1, hour=0, minute=0, second=0, microsecond=0))
    return float(cfg["DAILY_SPEND_CAP_USD"]) - day, float(cfg["MONTHLY_SPEND_CAP_USD"]) - month
```

### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T11:36

You don't need to raise it, and my "raise the cap first" option was wrong. I checked the ledger, and your cap isn't the problem.

- **Where the $14.56 went:** a single ledger row at 09:44 today booked **$14.29** for 57,807 seconds (about 16 hours) of one pod. That is the overnight pod that stayed up. My four runs today cost $0.07, $0.09 and $0.11, plus the refused attempt at $0.
- **Why the pod was refused:** $15.00 minus $14.56 leaves $0.44, and the desk refuses to start a pod with less than $0.50 left (`renderdesk/workbench.py`, `_wb_left`). My next run needs only about $0.10, but the rule still blocks it.
- **What this means:**
  - The cap is fine. It did its job and stopped further spend after that overnight pod.
  - The count resets at midnight, so a pod can start again tomorrow with nothing changed.

So I'll rebuild the orbit by eye on frame sheets now, with no pod. If you want a render tonight, raising the cap for one day is your call, and I won't do it.

<details><summary>tool result</summary>

```
def orbit_flyback_arc(boy, mom, wide_eye, wide_aim, n1=19, n2=5, n3=19, start_az=60.0, angle=230.0, d_orbit=1.3,
                      el_orbit=-12.0, arc_angle=-33.0, arc_close=0.46, lens=LENS):
    """The reference's move from "over" to "rope" (2.73-4.13 s), read frame by frame on 14 Nov 2026:
      1 whip orbit (n1 frames): round the pair from low, starting in front of the boy (start_az) and turning
        `angle` degrees (+ = counter-clockwise from above, by his left side) while the aim slides from the
        boy to Mom; fast in the middle, eased at both ends (reference: about 270 deg in 0.6 s, by eye).
      2 fly-back (n2 frames): straight to the wide spot (wide_eye, looking at wide_aim), very fast
        (reference: medium to small in about 4 frames, through the skeleton's legs, by eye).
      3 arc in (n3 frames): from the wide spot, circle Mom by `arc_angle` degrees while closing to
        `arc_close` of the distance (reference COLMAP solve 3.53-3.97 s: 33 deg, x0.46, a slight drop).
    Angles and lengths are settings because parts 1 and 2 are by eye: they are tuned by matching the clip
    analyzer's readings of our render to the reference's."""
    out = []
    p1 = progress(n1, floor=0.25)
    for t in p1:
        aim = boy.lerp(mom, t)
        eye = around(aim, start_az + angle * t, el_orbit, d_orbit)
        out.append((eye, look(eye, aim), lens))
    e1, a1 = out[-1][0], mom.copy()
    for t in progress(n2, floor=0.1):
        eye = e1.lerp(wide_eye, t)
        aim = a1.lerp(wide_aim, t)
        out.append((eye, look(eye, aim), lens))
    v = wide_eye - mom
    d0 = v.length
    az0 = math.degrees(math.atan2(v.y, v.x))
    el0 = math.degrees(math.asin(max(-1.0, min(1.0, v.z / d0))))
    for t in progress(n3, floor=0.3):
        aim = wide_aim.lerp(mom, min(1.0, t * 1.5))
        eye = around(mom, az0 + arc_angle * t, el0 - 4.0 * t, d0 * (1 + (arc_close - 1) * t))
        out.append((eye, look(eye, aim), lens))
    return out
```

```
def head_of(root):
    """World position of the 'head' bone's base, else 93% up the tallest mesh under root."""
    stack, arm = [root], None
    while stack:
        o = stack.pop()
        if o.type == "ARMATURE":
            arm = o
            break
        stack += list(o.children)
    if arm and "head" in arm.pose.bones:
        return arm.matrix_world @ arm.pose.bones["head"].head
    zs = [(o.matrix_world @ Vector(c)).z for o in root.children_recursive if o.type == "MESH" for c in o.bound_box]
    return Vector((root.location.x, root.location.y, 0.93 * max(zs)))


def facing(deg):
    a = math.radians(deg)
    return Vector((math.sin(a), -math.cos(a), 0.0))      # 0 faces -Y (recipes/human.build_human)


BH, MH = head_of(boy) + Vector((0, 0, 0.06)), head_of(mom) + Vector((0, 0, 0.06))   # about eye level
FB, FM = facing(BOY_FACING), facing(MOM_FACING)
TOE = Vector((0.55, 0.88, PT + 0.05))
up = Vector((0, 0, 1))
mid = (BH + MH) / 2
side = (MH - BH).cross(up).normalized()
if side.y > 0:
    side = -side                                          # the side toward the room (-Y)
rot = lambda v, deg: Matrix.Rotation(math.radians(deg), 3, "Z") @ v
FM_CAM = rot(FM, 25.0)                                    # mom's front cameras sit off the line to the boy
DMB = Vector(((BH - MH).x, (BH - MH).y, 0.0)).normalized()  # from mom toward the boy
PERP = DMB.cross(up).normalized()
if PERP.y > 0:
    PERP = -PERP
print("HEADS", tuple(round(v, 3) for v in BH), tuple(round(v, 3) for v in MH))

# name, nominal first analyzer frame, eye, aim, lens (mm, vertical 36 mm sensor). The shot starts

# finish the previous shot (whip out of it).
```

```
# Imagine — Visual Creation Suite

## Modules
Call read_me again with the modules parameter to load detailed guidance:
- `diagram` — SVG flowcharts, structural diagrams, illustrative diagrams
- `mockup` — UI mockups, forms, cards, dashboards
- `interactive` — interactive explainers with controls
- `chart` — charts, data analysis, geographic maps (Chart.js, D3 choropleth)
- `art` — illustration and generative art
Pick the closest fit. The module includes all relevant design guidance.

**Complexity budget — hard limits:**
- Box subtitles: ≤5 words. Detail goes in click-through (`sendPrompt`) or the prose below — not the box.
- Colors: ≤2 ramps per diagram. If colors encode meaning (states, tiers), add a 1-line legend. Otherwise use one neutral ramp.
- Horizontal tier: ≤4 boxes at full width (~140px each). 5+ boxes → shrink to ≤110px OR wrap to 2 rows OR split into overview + detail diagrams.

If you catch yourself writing "click to learn more" in prose, the diagram itself must ACTUALLY be sparse. Don't promise brevity then front-load everything.

**Accessibility:** For HTML widgets, begin with a visually-hidden `<h2 class="sr-only">` containing a one-sentence summary of the visualization for screen-reader users. (SVG widgets use `role="img"` with `<title>` and `<desc>` instead — see SVG setup.)

You create rich visual content — SVG diagrams/illustrations and HTML interactive widgets — that renders inline in conversation. The best output feels like a natural extension of the chat.

## Core Design System

These rules apply to ALL use cases.

### Philosophy
- **Seamless**: Users shouldn't notice where claude.ai ends and your widget begins.
- **Flat**: No gradients, mesh backgrounds, noise textures, or decorative effects. Clean flat surfaces.
- **Compact**: Show the essential inline. Explain the rest in text.
- **Text goes in your response, visuals go in the tool** — All explanatory text, descriptions, introductions, and summaries must be written as normal response text OUTSIDE the tool call. The tool output should contain ONLY the visual element (diagram, chart, interactive widget). Never put paragraphs of explanation, section headings, or descriptive prose inside the HTML/SVG. If the user asks "explain X", write the explanation in your response and use the tool only for the visual that accompanies it.

### Streaming
Output streams token-by-token. Structure code so useful content appears early.
- **HTML**: `<style>` (short) → content HTML → `<script>` last.
- **SVG**: `<defs>` (markers) → visual elements immediately.
- Prefer inline `style="..."` over `<style>` blocks — inputs/controls must look correct mid-stream.
- Keep `<style>` under ~15 lines. Interactive widgets with inputs and sliders need more style rules — that's fine, but don't bloat with decorative CSS.
- Gradients, shadows, and blur flash during streaming DOM diffs. Use solid flat fills instead.

### Rules
- No `<!-- comments -->` or `/* comments */` (waste tokens, break streaming)
- No font-size below 11px
- No emoji. Icons = Tabler **outline** webfont (5800+, already loaded): `<i class="ti ti-home"></i>`. Outline only — never use `-filled` suffixes (`ti-heart-filled` etc. are not loaded and will render blank). Inherits color + font-size from parent. Decorative icons get `aria-hidden="true"`; icon-only buttons get `aria-label`. Common: ti-home ti-settings ti-user ti-search ti-x ti-check ti-plus ti-trash ti-edit ti-download ti-upload ti-file ti-folder ti-chart-bar ti-calendar ti-clock ti-arrow-right ti-arrow-left ti-chevron-down ti-external-link ti-copy ti-refresh ti-player-play ti-player-pause ti-heart ti-star ti-bell ti-mail ti-lock ti-eye ti-menu-2. Don't hand-draw icon SVG paths.
- No gradients, drop shadows, blur, glow, or neon effects
- No dark/colored backgrounds on outer containers (transparent only — host provides the bg)
- **Typography**: The default font is the person's Interface font, usually Anthropic Sans. For the rare editorial/blockquote moment, use `fon ...[cut]
```

```
"""Solve every shot of the reference from 2 to 10 s with COLMAP (BSD), one model per shot.

Usage: python solve_shots.py WORK VIDEO
For each shot (frame ranges from runs/021 build.py's shot starts): frames at 540x960 with the caption band
masked, then COLMAP with the lens fixed at the opening shot's solved value (961.4 px focal; short shots
cannot work out a lens on their own) and a small starting angle allowed (short moves). Writes
WORK/<shot>/path.json: solved cameras, the scene's up, the anchor (solved points in the middle of the
middle frame: the shot's subject) and quality numbers. A shot that does not solve is reported, not faked.
"""
import json
import os
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys

import cv2
import numpy as np
import pycolmap

WORK, VIDEO = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
SHOTS = {"S2": (60, 68), "S3": (69, 96), "S4": (97, 101), "S5": (102, 119), "S6": (120, 166), "S7": (167, 175),
         "S8": (176, 199), "S9": (200, 225), "S10": (226, 280), "S11": (281, 302)}
FOCAL = "961.431,270,480,0.1795"

allf = os.path.join(WORK, "all")
os.makedirs(allf, exist_ok=True)
if not os.listdir(allf):
    subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-v", "error", "-y", "-i", VIDEO, "-vf", "select=lt(n\\,303),scale=540:960", "-vsync", "0",
                    "-start_number", "0", os.path.join(allf, "f%04d.png")], check=True)
report = {}
for shot, (a, b) in SHOTS.items():
    D = os.path.join(WORK, shot)
    shutil.rmtree(D, ignore_errors=True)
    os.makedirs(f"{D}/images")
    os.makedirs(f"{D}/masks")
    for f in range(a, b + 1):
        shutil.copy(f"{allf}/f{f:04d}.png", f"{D}/images/f{f:04d}.png")
        m = np.full((960, 540), 255, np.uint8)
        m[int(0.70 * 960):int(0.80 * 960), :] = 0
        cv2.imwrite(f"{D}/masks/f{f:04d}.png.png", m)
    db = f"{D}/db.db"
    reader = pycolmap.ImageReaderOptions()
    reader.mask_path = f"{D}/masks"
    reader.camera_model = "SIMPLE_RADIAL"
    reader.camera_params = FOCAL
    pycolmap.extract_features(db, f"{D}/images", camera_mode=pycolmap.CameraMode.SINGLE, reader_options=reader)
    pairing = pycolmap.SequentialPairingOptions()
    pairing.overlap = 12
    pycolmap.match_sequential(db, pairing_options=pairing)
    opt = pycolmap.IncrementalPipelineOptions()
    opt.ba_refine_focal_length = False
    opt.ba_refine_extra_params = False
    opt.min_num_matches = 15
    opt.mapper.init_min_tri_angle = 2.0
    opt.mapper.init_min_num_inliers = 50
    os.makedirs(f"{D}/sparse", exist_ok=True)
    try:
        recs = pycolmap.incremental_mapping(db, f"{D}/images", f"{D}/sparse", options=opt)
    except Exception as e:
        recs = {}
        print(shot, "mapping error", e)
    n = b - a + 1
    if not recs:
        report[shot] = dict(frames=n, solved=0)
        print(f"{shot} frames {a}-{b}: NOT SOLVED")
        continue
    rec = max(recs.values(), key=lambda r: r.num_reg_images())
    path = {}
    for im in rec.images.values():
        if im.has_pose:
            pose = im.cam_from_world() if callable(im.cam_from_world) else im.cam_from_world
            R = pose.rotation.matrix()
            path[int(im.name[1:5])] = dict(center=(-R.T @ pose.translation).tolist(), R=R.tolist())
    fr = sorted(path)
    up = -np.mean([np.array(path[f]["R"]).T @ np.array([0, 1.0, 0]) for f in fr], axis=0)
    up /= np.linalg.norm(up)
    mid = fr[len(fr) // 2]
    im = next(i for i in rec.images.values() if i.name == f"f{mid:04d}.png")
    pts = [rec.points3D[p.point3D_id].xyz for p in im.points2D
           if p.has_point3D() and 0.30 * 540 <= p.xy[0] <= 0.70 * 540 and 0.25 * 960 <= p.xy[1] <= 0.70 * 960]
```

### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T11:56

Two things are running and one result is in.

- **Result so far:** the new take solver (`tools/camera_solve/solve_takes.py`) registered 14 of 32 frames for take B (frames 69 to 100). The old per-shot solve got 7 of 28 there. The whip frames 83 to 100 still don't register, because the blur leaves too few matches to find a camera.
- **Running now:** the long take, frames 102 to 301, on the box. It needs no pod, so it costs nothing, and I'll report the number of frames solved when it finishes.
- **Round 3, the orbit renders:** the fixed pod run should be done, and I'll show its sheet when I'm back at it.

**What this means for the new loop:** COLMAP will give a real path through the sharp parts. For the blurred whip stretches, I'll fill the gap by estimating the pure camera turn between the nearest solved frames with OpenCV. Fast whips are mostly rotation, and rotation can be measured even when 3D points can't be matched.


### USER 2026-11-15T11:56

do we need to build some code on top of the algorithms to make them better?


### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T11:56

Yes. The two algorithms give raw readings, and the gap between those and a camera you'd accept is where the missing code is.

**What I know from today:**
- **COLMAP:** it gives a real 3D path, but only on sharp frames, since the blurred whip frames 83 to 100 didn't register.
- **The analyzer's 2D numbers:** copying them straight in made a zig-zag. The pan direction flipped about 3.1 times per second in our render against 2.0 in the reference.

**The code I'd build on top, in order:**
1. **Whip bridge:** it measures the turn between the sharp frames on each side of a whip with OpenCV, and joins the COLMAP segments into one continuous path. This is untested, so I don't know yet how accurate it is.
2. **Path to our scene:** it puts the solved path onto our characters (position, scale, aim at the subject), so the camera follows the path automatically with no hand-set numbers.
3. **Gatekeeper:** it rejects a path with flips, jumps or a lost subject before the pod ever sees it. That would have caught the typo and the zig-zag.

🔴 **NEEDED:** go ahead with all three, starting with the whip bridge? **My pick is yes.** The long-take solve is still running first, and I'll use its result.

- **Still different:** by the end (frames 118 to 124) the boy has left the frame and Mom stands in profile at the edge. The reference has both in a medium shot, with Mom facing the camera. Part of that is how the characters are posed, not the camera.

### USER 2026-11-15T11:58

sure but how would you build the code?


### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T11:58

**Three small tools in a row, each one tested before the next uses it.** The key is that each tool gets a test against something we already know, not my eye.

- **1 Whip bridge** (`tools/camera_path.py`): it takes the sharp-frame poses from COLMAP and fills each whip gap by chaining the frame-to-frame turn that OpenCV measures. The chain is pinned to land exactly on the next solved frame, so errors can't pile up. **Test first:** delete 10 sharp frames that COLMAP did solve, bridge them, and compare with COLMAP's own poses, so we know the error in degrees before trusting it on a real whip.
- **2 Path to our scene** (`recipes/path_to_blender.py`): it puts each take's path on our characters (anchor on the subject, scale from the subject's size, up = Z, lens 36.1 mm) and writes the camera for every frame. This replaces the hand-built shot plan, so no hand editing remains. **Test:** project the subject through the placed camera and check it stays where the reference has it.
- **3 Gatekeeper** (`tools/path_gate.py`): it rejects a path before the pod sees it if it flips direction more than the reference does, jumps between frames, or loses the subject. It runs on the Mac for the numbers and on the kept-up pod for the geometry checks.

**Estimate, not measured:** about 1.5 to 2 hours for the three, with the bridge test as the first checkpoint.

I can draw this as a one-page diagram with a real example if you want it before I start.

- tool `Write`: `{"file_path": "/Users/nick/Projects/clip-analyzer/tools/camera_path.py", "content": "\"\"\"Whip bridge: one camera pose for EVERY frame of a take, from COLMAP poses on the sharp frames plus OpenCV\nfor the blurred frames in between (15 Nov 2026).\n\nCOLMAP registers sharp frames only. A whip is mostly the camera turning on the spot, and a turn can be measured\nwithout 3D points: for a pure rotation the picture moves by the homography H = K R K^-1, so R = K^-1 H K.\nBridge(a, b): the frames a and b have COLMAP poses, the frames between do not.\n  1 frame-to-frame turn between neighbours (SIFT + RANSAC homography; fallback ECC on a small copy for heavy blur)\n  2 chain the turns from a; at b the chain misses COLMAP's pose by an error rotation E; spread E evenly over the\n    gap (a fraction k/n of it at step k), so the bridge starts and ends exactly on COLMAP's poses\n  3 camera centre: smooth interpolation between the two solved centres (a whip is mostly turning, not travelling)\nPoses use COLMAP's convention: R = world to camera, centre = -R^T t.\n\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport math\n\nimport cv2\nimport numpy as np\n\nW, H = 540, 960\nFOCAL = 961.431\nK = np.array([[FOCAL, 0, W / 2], [0, FOCAL, H / 2], [0, 0, 1.0]])\nKI = np.linalg.inv(K)\nCAPTION = (int(0.70 * H), int(0.80 * H))               # rows to ignore (burned-in captions)\n\n\ndef _gray(img):\n    g = cv2.cvtColor(img, cv2.COLOR_BGR2GRAY) if img.ndim == 3 else img\n    g = g.copy()\n    g[CAPTION[0]:CAPTION[1], :] = 0\n    return g\n\n\ndef _nearest_rotation(M):\n    U, _, Vt = np.linalg.svd(M)\n    R = U @ Vt\n    if np.linalg.det(R) < 0:\n        R = U @ np.diag([1, 1, -1]) @ Vt\n    return R\n\n\ndef turn_between(img_a, img_b):\n    \"\"\"Rotation taking camera-a coordinates to camera-b coordinates (x_b = R x_a), the method used, and a quality number.\n    Returns (None, 'fail', 0) when nothing can be measured.\"\"\"\n    ga, gb = _gray(img_a), _gray(img_b)\n    mask = np.full(ga.shape, 255, np.uint8)\n    mask[CAPTION[0]:CAPTION[1], :] = 0\n    sift = cv2.SIFT_create(nfeatures=4000)\n    ka, da = sift.detectAndCompute(ga, mask)\n    kb, db = sift.detectAndCompute(gb, mask)\n    if da is not None and db is not None and len(ka) > 20 and len(kb) > 20:\n        m = cv2.BFMatcher().knnMatch(da, db, k=2)\n        good = [x[0] for x in m if len(x) == 2 and x[0].distance < 0.75 * x[1].distance]\n        if len(good) >= 12:\n            pa = np.float32([ka[g.queryIdx].pt for g in good])\n            pb = np.float32([kb[g.trainIdx].pt for g in good])\n            Hm, inl = cv2.findHomography(pa, pb, cv2.RANSAC, 3.0)\n            if Hm is not None and inl.sum() >= 12:\n                return _nearest_rotation(KI @ Hm @ K), \"sift\", int(inl.sum())\n    s = 4                                                      # heavy blur: ECC on a quarter-size copy\n    sa, sb = cv2.resize(ga, (W // s, H // s)), cv2.resize(gb, (W // s, H // s))\n    warp = np.eye(3, dtype=np.float32)\n    try:\n        cc, warp`
- tool `Write`: `{"file_path": "/Users/nick/Projects/clip-analyzer/tools/camera_solve/bridge_test.py", "content": "\"\"\"Test of the whip bridge against COLMAP itself (15 Nov 2026).\n\nTake frames that COLMAP DID solve, pretend a block of them is a whip gap (drop their poses), bridge the block with\ntools/camera_path.bridge, and measure the angle between the bridged rotation and COLMAP's own rotation for every\ndropped frame. Also reports the chain's end error E (how far the OpenCV chain alone drifted before being pinned).\nUsage: python bridge_test.py MODELS.json FRAMES_DIR   (FRAMES_DIR: f0000.png ... at 540x960)\nPrints one line per gap size and block; the numbers are the evidence, nothing here is tuned to pass.\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport os\nimport sys\n\nimport cv2\nimport numpy as np\n\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), \"..\"))\nimport camera_path as cp\n\nmodels = json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))[\"models\"]\nFR = sys.argv[2]\nrows = []\nfor m in models:\n    P = {int(f): v for f, v in m[\"path\"].items()}\n    fs = sorted(P)\n    for gap in (5, 10, 18):                                   # 5 = the short gaps, 18 = the long whip (83-100)\n        for start in range(fs[0], fs[-1] - gap - 1, max(gap, 12)):\n            a, b = start, start + gap + 1\n            if a not in P or b not in P or any(f not in P for f in range(a, b + 1)):\n                continue\n            imgs = {f: cv2.imread(os.path.join(FR, f\"f{f:04d}.png\")) for f in range(a, b + 1)}\n            out, drift = cp.bridge(P[a], P[b], list(range(a + 1, b)), imgs)\n            err = [cp.angle_between(out[f][\"R\"], P[f][\"R\"]) for f in range(a + 1, b)]\n            cen = [float(np.linalg.norm(np.array(out[f][\"center\"]) - np.array(P[f][\"center\"]))) for f in range(a + 1, b)]\n            span = float(np.linalg.norm(np.array(P[b][\"center\"]) - np.array(P[a][\"center\"])))\n            hows = [out[f][\"how\"] for f in range(a + 1, b)]\n            rows.append((gap, a, b, drift, max(err), float(np.mean(err)), max(cen), span, hows.count(\"sift\"), hows.count(\"ecc\"), hows.count(\"hold\")))\n            print(f\"gap {gap:2d} frames {a}-{b}: chain drift before pinning {drift:5.1f} deg | bridged-vs-COLMAP rotation error mean {np.mean(err):5.2f} max {max(err):5.2f} deg\"\n                  f\" | centre error max {max(cen):.3f} (solved centres {span:.3f} apart) | turns by sift/ecc/hold {hows.count('sift')}/{hows.count('ecc')}/{hows.count('hold')}\")\nfor gap in (5, 10, 18):\n    r = [x for x in rows if x[0] == gap]\n    if r:\n        print(f\"SUMMARY gap {gap}: {len(r)} blocks, mean error {np.mean([x[5] for x in r]):.2f} deg, worst {max(x[4] for x in r):.2f} deg\")\n"}`

### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T12:01

**Motion graph:** sent above, with `orbit_Q3` in orange and the reference in blue. It covers frames 82 to 124 in three panels (zoom, sideways, up/down), and the grey bands mark the reference's whip frames.

**What the graph shows:**
- **Direction:** from frame 83 to 97 the reference whips to the right, with sideways speed up to +529 %/s (the panel is clipped at +150). Ours swings left, down to -379 %/s, so it moves the wrong way for the whole first half.
- **The pull-out spikes:** the reference's two big zoom-out spikes at frames 101 to 104 (down to -524 %/s) are missing from ours, which stays flat at 0.
- **The end:** both zoom in after frame 106, but ours only reaches +144 %/s against +399 %/s in the reference.

**Bayesian learning:** yes for the camera path, not yet for the review.
- **For the path:** it fits well. Our readings are noisy but always there (OpenCV), or exact but missing in whips (COLMAP). A Bayesian smoother such as a Kalman or RTS filter weighs each reading by how much it can be trusted, and it gives the path an uncertainty at every frame. It would replace the hand-made pinning in the whip bridge, and it is my pick for the bridge's next version.
- **For your verdicts:** with one clip and one reviewer there is little data, which is where Bayesian methods do best. But I'd wait until the path part exists, since there is nothing yet to tune from your verdicts.

The bridge test is still running in the background, and I'll report its error numbers when it finishes.

### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T12:03

**Identical graphs would mean the same speed, direction and timing of motion, which is a good target, but I wouldn't use it as the recipe.**

- **We already tried copying the graph:** the first driven render matched the reference's pan graph at 0.93 correlation (from run 021's `review.md`) and still looked wrong to you. The matching render later flipped pan direction 3.1 times a second, against 2.0 in the reference.
- **Why it fails:** the graph is the analyzer's noisy 2D reading of the reference, so copying it also copies the measurement noise as if it were real motion. The same pan speed also looks different in our room, because the distances and characters differ.
- **Where it does help:** as a gate. After we rebuild the path from the real COLMAP solve, I render it and read it back through the same analyzer, and the graphs should land close. That catches a wrong direction or a missing pull-out, as in Q3 today.
- **What it can't tell:** whether both people are in frame, or whether it feels smooth. That stays your review.

**My pick:** build the path from the 3D solve, use the graph match as an automatic gate, and keep you as the final judge.

### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T12:05

The identical-graph attempt is running, and the bridge test results are in.

**Identical-graph loop, as set up:**
- **Procedure:** it renders our clip, reads the render back through the same analyzer, adds the difference from the reference to the camera commands, and re-renders. It does 3 rounds at gain 0.7, and I'll show each round's graph.
- **Round 0, the render we already have:** zoom correlation 0.70, pan 0.49, tilt 0.28, picture-change correlation 0.55, pan flips 3.1 per second.
- **Time:** it should take 20 to 30 minutes, mostly the pod renders and the analyzer scans. The pod stays up between rounds.

**Bridge test result (important for the path plan):**
- **Errors:** on frames COLMAP solved and I held out, the OpenCV bridge was off by about 5° on average for 5-frame gaps, about 8° for 10 frames and about 12° for 18 frames, with worst cases of 20°, 50° and 62°.
- **What this means:** that is too inaccurate to trust. The chain drifted 4° to 39° before pinning.
- **Likely cause:** the camera also travels, not only turns, and moving people spoil the matches. That is my guess, not tested.

So the whip bridge as I designed it doesn't work well enough yet. I'll report the graph loop first, and then we decide how to fix the bridge, probably with the Bayesian fusion you suggested.


### USER 2026-11-15T12:06

our render or the clips render?


### CLAUDE 2026-11-15T12:06

Both, depending on which part you mean:

- **Graph loop:** it renders **our** clip in Blender on the pod, with our characters. It reads that render back through the analyzer and compares its graph with the reference clip's graph. The reference is never rendered, because its graph is the analyzer's reading of the real video.
- **Bridge test:** it ran only on **the reference clip's frames**, the ones COLMAP had solved. Our render wasn't involved, because the test checks how well OpenCV can recover a camera turn we already know.
